\alpha(\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)})\in\Ext^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}/\mathcal{O}_{X})
is zero. By
Theorem

2.8
, this class maps to the intermediate obstruction
\Omega^{\prime}(\mathcal{A},\sigma)
, which is therefore also zero.

Conversely, suppose
\Omega^{\prime}(\mathcal{A},\sigma)=0
. This means there is a section
\bar{\ell}\in(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X})(X)
which maps to
1\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-2.15277pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptm\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}(X)
. Hence, we may use the image of
\bar{\ell}
under the map
(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X})(X)\rightarrow(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma})(X)
to define a semi-trace
f
. Now, invoking
Theorem

2.8
again, the section
\bar{\ell}
corresponds to a right-splitting of the sequence (
Lie
) for this
f
. This concludes the proof.
∎

3.
Relative deformations of quadratic triples

Let
X
be an arbitrary scheme and let
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
be a quadratic triple on
X
. In this section we analyze the deformations of
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
in comparison with the deformations of
\mathcal{A}
. We say that quadratic triples are
relatively unobstructed
when they deform if and only if the underlying algebra deforms. We show that, in two extraordinary cases, certain quadratic triples are always relatively unobstructed.

Specifically, in
Theorem

3.3
, we show that any quaternion Azumaya algebra with quadratic pair is relatively unobstructed as are all quadratic triples over a scheme
X
on which
2
is a global unit. Both results follow from observing that in these cases, the Lie algebra sheaf
\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}
is a direct summand of
\mathfrak{pgl}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
X
be an arbitrary scheme. Let
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
be a quadratic triple on
X
and assume that
\deg(\mathcal{A})=2
. Then
\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}
is a direct summand of
\mathfrak{pgl}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proof.

From the descriptions of the Lie algebra sheaves given in
Section

1.B.ii
, we know that

\mathfrak{pgl}_{2}=\Mat_{2n}(\mathcal{O}_{X})/\mathcal{O}_{X}

where for a general matrix we have

\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\equiv\begin{bmatrix}0&b\\
c&d-a\end{bmatrix}\text{ as sections in }\Mat_{2n}(\mathcal{O}_{X})/\mathcal{O}_{X}.

Therefore, we can write for
U\subseteq X
open

\mathfrak{pgl}_{2}(U)=\left\{\begin{bmatrix}0&a\\
b&c\end{bmatrix}\middle|\,a,b,c\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\right\}\\

and then compute that

\mathfrak{pgo}_{2}(U)=\left\{\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
0&c\end{bmatrix}\middle|\,c\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\right\}

Thus there is a splitting
\mathfrak{pgl}_{2}=\mathfrak{pgo}_{2}\oplus\mathcal{N}
where

\mathcal{N}(U)=\left\{\begin{bmatrix}0&a\\
b&0\end{bmatrix}\middle|\,a,b\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\right\}.\\

Now, any automorphism
\varphi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{2}(U)
induces an automorphism
\overline{\varphi}
on
\mathfrak{pgl}_{2}(U)
which stabilizes
\mathfrak{pgo}_{2}(U)
. We argue that, in addition, any
\overline{\varphi}
also stabilizes
\mathcal{N}(U)
. Since any element of
\mathcal{N}(U)
is (represented by) a symmetric matrix
s
with
f_{2}(s)=0
, the image
\varphi(s)
must have the same properties. Thus we have that

\varphi(s)=\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&a\end{bmatrix}

with
a=0
since
f(\varphi(s))=a
. Thus
\varphi(s)\in\mathcal{N}(U)
. The fact that all automorphisms in
\mathbf{PGO}_{2}
stabilize
\mathcal{N}
implies our claim as follows.

Let
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
be an étale cover splitting
\mathcal{A}
and let
(\varphi_{ij})\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbf{PGO}_{2})
be a cocycle defining
\mathcal{A}
. Then
(\overline{\varphi_{ij}})\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbf{Aut}(\mathfrak{pgo}_{2}))
will define
\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}
and the same cocycle will define
\mathfrak{pgl}_{\mathcal{A}}
when viewed as an element of
Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbf{Aut}(\mathfrak{pgl}_{2}))
. Letting
\pi_{i}\colon\mathfrak{pgl}_{\mathcal{A}}|_{U_{i}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{N}|_{U_{i}}
be the canonical projections, the following diagrams will commute since
\mathcal{N}
is stabilized:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{pgo}_{2}|_{U_{i}}\oplus\mathcal{N}|_{U_{i}})|_{U_{ij}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{pgo}_{2}|_{U_{j}}\oplus\mathcal{N}|_{U_{j}})|_{U_{ij}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{pgo}_{2}|_{U_{i}})|_{U_{ij}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{pgo}_{2}|_{U_{j}})|_{U_{ij}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}|_{U_{ij}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\varphi_{ij}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{j}|_{U_{ij}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\varphi_{ij}}}

Thus, the
\pi_{i}
glue into a global projection
\pi\colon\mathfrak{pgl}_{\mathcal{A}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}
which splits the inclusion
\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{pgl}_{\mathcal{A}}
, and we are done.
∎

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
X
be a scheme such that
2
is invertible on
X
, i.e.
2\in\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times}(X)
. Let
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
be a quadratic triple on
X
. Then
\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}\cong\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}
. In particular, the inclusion
\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}\subset\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X}
realizes
\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}
as a direct summand of
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X}
.

Proof.

Consider the composition

\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X}

of the canonical inclusion and the quotient. This map is injective and one can check that
\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}\subset\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}
. For any point
x\in X
, the induced map

(\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma})_{\kappa(x)}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma)})_{\kappa(x)}

is an isomorphism since
\kappa(x)
is of characteristic not
2
. Thus
\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}\cong\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma)}
.

Finally, the exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}\rightarrow 0

is left-split by the map
(1/2)(\mathrm{Id}-\sigma)
.
∎

The following theorem shows how to use
3.1
and
3.2
to obtain statements on the relative unobstructedness of quadratic triples.

Theorem 3.3
.

Let
k
be any field and let
X
be any scheme that’s separated over
k
. Let
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
be a quadratic triple on
X
. If either:

(i)

k
is not characteristic
2
, or

(ii)

\mathcal{A}
is a quaternion algebra,

then for any small extension

0\rightarrow J\rightarrow C^{\prime}\rightarrow C\rightarrow 0

of Artinian local
k
-algebras
(C^{\prime},\mathfrak{m}_{C^{\prime}})
and
(C,\mathfrak{m}_{C})
with residue fields
k
, the outer vertical arrows of the commutative diagram below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)})\otimes_{k}J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}(C^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)})\otimes_{k}J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X})\otimes_{k}J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{A},Az}(C^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{A},Az}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X})\otimes_{k}J}

are injective.

Consequently, if
\xi=(\mathcal{A}_{C},\sigma_{C},f_{C})
is any deformation of
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
to
X_{C}
, then the obstruction
ob(\xi;X_{C^{\prime}}/X_{C})
to deforming
\xi
to
X_{C^{\prime}}
vanishes if and only if the obstruction
ob(\mathcal{A}_{C};X_{C^{\prime}}/X_{C})
to deforming
\mathcal{A}_{C}
as an Azumaya algebra vanishes.

Proof.

We obtain the commutative diagram in the statement from
Corollary

1.19
. The outer vertical arrows are injective since they have a left inverse arising from the splitting

\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}\rightarrow\mathfrak{pgl}_{\mathcal{A}}=\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}

which exists either by
Lemma

3.2
in case
(i)
or
Lemma

3.1
in case
(ii)
.
∎

As a brief warning, when we have a quadratic triple
(\mathcal{A}_{C},\sigma_{C},f_{C})
over
X_{C}
as in
Theorem

3.3
and we assume that there exists a deformation
\mathcal{A}_{C^{\prime}}
on
X_{C^{\prime}}
of
\mathcal{A}_{C}
as an Azumaya algebra, we are guaranteed that there exists a deformation
(\mathcal{B}_{C^{\prime}},\sigma_{C^{\prime}},f_{C^{\prime}})
on
X_{C^{\prime}}
of the quadratic triple, but the argument does not imply that
\mathcal{A}_{C^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{B}_{C^{\prime}}
.

For the other extreme, when
X
is purely of characteristic
2
, the following lemma and subsequent theorem show that the situation is completely different.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
2
.
Let
X
be a smooth and projective surface over
k
. Assume that the canonical bundle of
X
is trivial, i.e.
K_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Fix an Azumaya algebra with locally quadratic orthogonal involution
(\mathcal{A},\sigma)
over
X
such that the map

\mathcal{A}(X)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}+\sigma}\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}(X)

is identically zero.

Then, with these assumptions, the canonical map

(20)

\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}/\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X})

is identically zero as well.

Proof.

Since
\sigma
is assumed locally quadratic, we have
\mathcal{O}_{X}\subset\mathcal{S}\hskip-2.15277pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptm\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}=\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}
. Consider, then, the canonical commutative square of sheaves below.

(21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}

Associated to (
21
), there is an induced commutative diagram of
k
-vector spaces

(22)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}/\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X}).}

By Serre duality, the square (
22
) is canonically isomorphic with the square below, gotten by dualizing the square of global sections of the dual of (
21
):

(23)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,(\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma})^{\vee})^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathcal{A}^{\vee})^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,(\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}/\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\vee})^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\vee})^{\vee}.}

Now, the dual of the canonical inclusion
\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}\subset\mathcal{A}
can be identified using the isomorphism
\mathcal{A}\cong\mathcal{A}^{\vee}
coming from the reduced trace, and the isomorphism
\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}\cong\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}^{\vee}
coming from the existence of a nondegenerate bilinear form
b_{-}
, see
[
GNR25
, 2.7 Lemma (ii)]
. The composition

\mathcal{A}\cong\mathcal{A}^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}^{\vee}\cong\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}

is the map defined on sections by
x\mapsto x+\sigma(x)
. Because of our assumptions, we have that the top arrow in (
23
) is therefore zero. Since the left vertical arrow of (
23
) is a surjection, it follows that the bottom horizontal arrow of (
23
) is zero too.
∎

Theorem 3.5
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
2
.
Let
X
be a smooth and projective surface over
k
. Assume that the canonical bundle of
X
is trivial, i.e.
K_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Lastly, fix an Azumaya algebra with quadratic pair
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
over
X
such that both of the maps

(24)

\mathcal{A}(X)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}+\sigma}\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}(X)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{S}\hskip-2.15277pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptm}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}(X)\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)

are identically zero.

Then, with these assumptions, the canonical map

(25)

\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X})

is identically zero as well.

Proof.

The Lie algebra sheaf
\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}
sits in a canonical extension

0\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}/\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow 0.

Consider the commutative ladder with exact rows whose first column is the above extension. Here we define
\mathcal{N}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}
to be the appropriate locally free cokernel sheaf.

(26)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}

By the snake lemma, the kernel of the map
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)}
is isomorphic with
\mathcal{O}_{X}
. The inclusion of this kernel
\mathcal{O}_{X}\subset\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}
is identified with the map sending
1
to the element
\ell\in\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}(X)
with
\ell+\sigma(\ell)=1
which defines
f
. There is an induced commutative diagram with exact rows and columns as below, obtained by applying cohomology to (
26
).

(27)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}/\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{N}_{(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

By
Lemma

3.4
, the map
\varphi
in (
27
) is zero. Hence
\psi
in (
27
) is an isomorphism. If we could show that
\tau
is also zero, it then follows that the map
\rho
is zero as well, as claimed in the theorem statement.

By Serre duality, the map
\tau
is dual to the map

\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma})^{\vee})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})

defined by sending a functional
g
to
g(\ell)
. Note also that the inclusion
\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}\subset\mathcal{A}
yields the exact sequence of sheaves

0\rightarrow(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma})^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}^{\vee}\rightarrow 0.

This implies that a functional
g
on
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{A}\hskip-0.43057pt\textit{$\ell$\hskip-1.29167ptt}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}
has the form
g=\mathrm{trd}_{\mathcal{A}}(a\cdot-)
for some
a\in\mathcal{A}(X)
. By our assumptions, we have
a+\sigma(a)=0
, i.e.
a\in\mathcal{S}\hskip-2.15277pt\textit{y\hskip-1.29167ptm}_{\mathcal{A},\sigma}(X)
. Hence, by the definition of the semi-trace
f
, we have

g(\ell)=\mathrm{trd}_{\mathcal{A}}(a\ell)=f(a).

Since we are assuming that
f
is trivial on global sections, we find that
\tau
is zero. As explained above, this concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
2
. Let
X
be a smooth, projective
k
-surface with
K_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Let
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
be a quadratic triple on
X
with
4\mid\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{A})
and suppose that
\mathcal{A}(X)=k
. Then
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
satisfies the assumptions of
Theorem

3.5
. Indeed,
1+\sigma(1)=0
trivially and
f(1)=0
by
[
GNR25
, Corollary 3.8]
.

Remark 3.7
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
2
. Let
X
be a smooth, projective
k
-surface with
K_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be two quaternion Azumaya algebras on
X
and suppose that
(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B})(X)=k
. In this case, there is a canonical quadratic triple
(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B},\sigma,f)
and an isomorphism
\mathfrak{pgo}_{(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B},\sigma,f)}\cong\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{O}_{X}
which follows from the stack equivalence of
Theorem

1.2
. Thus
Corollary

1.22
is a special case of
Theorem

3.5
.

4.
A quadratic pair with obstructed deformations

In this section we prove
Theorem

4.5
which shows that, in characteristic 2, there exist quadratic triples with nonvanishing obstructions to deformation such that when one forgets the quadratic pair, the underlying Azumaya algebra has vanishing obstruction.

4.A.
Quaternion algebras on an Igusa surface

Throughout the remainder of this text we fix a field
k
which is both algebraically closed and of characteristic
2
. We also fix a pair of ordinary elliptic curves
(E_{1},p_{1})
and
(E_{2},p_{2})
over
k
, which are allowed to be equal, and we set
Y=E_{1}\times_{k}E_{2}
. There is a free action of
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
on
Y
defined by letting the nonunit element act via the involution
i\colon Y\rightarrow Y
given by
(x,y)\mapsto(-x,y+t)
for the unique
2
-torsion point
p_{2}\neq t\in E_{2}[2](k)
. Set
\pi:Y\rightarrow X
to be the associated quotient of this action. By construction,
X
is an Igusa surface.

We are going to construct two quaternion Azumaya algebras on
X
. The first is constructed in the following way. Let

(28)

0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}(p_{1})\rightarrow 0

be the unique non-split extension on
E_{1}
(which was discussed in
Example

2.5
). The sheaf
\mathcal{V}_{1}
is an indecomposable locally free sheaf of rank 2. Pulling back to
Y
along the projection
\pi_{1}:Y=E_{1}\times E_{2}\rightarrow E_{1}
yields an extension

(29)

0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}\rightarrow\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}(p_{1}\times E_{2})\rightarrow 0.

Because we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}

where
i_{0}(x)=-x
, we have that
i^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1}))=\pi_{1}^{*}(i_{0}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1}))
. However, it is clear that
i_{0}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}})=\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}
and that
i_{0}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}(p_{1}))=\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}(p_{1})
because
i_{0}(p_{1})=p_{1}
, and so by the uniqueness of (
28
), we have that
i_{0}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1})=\mathcal{V}_{1}
. In turn, we conclude that

i^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1}))=\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1}).

We now use the étale cover
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
and glue the objects in (
28
) to themselves to define objects on
X
. First, we have a fiber product diagram

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

A sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
Y\sqcup Y
is of the form
\mathcal{F}(U\sqcup V)=\mathcal{F}_{1}(U)\times\mathcal{F}_{2}(V)
for two sheaves
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}
on
Y
. For such a sheaf, we write
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{1}\sqcup\mathcal{F}_{2}
. Gluing data for
\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1})
consists of an isomorphism

\textrm{pr}_{1}^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\alpha^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1}))

which satisfies the cocycle condition. However, we have that

\displaystyle\textrm{pr}_{1}^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1}))

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1})\sqcup\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1}),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1}))

\displaystyle=\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1})\sqcup i^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1}))=\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1})\sqcup\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1})

so we may simply take the identity as our cocycle.

For similar reasons, the identity is also a valid gluing data for
\mathcal{O}(p_{1}\times E_{2})
and of course for
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
. After using this gluing data to descend to
X
, we obtain an extension of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
–modules

(30)

0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}\rightarrow 0

where
\mathcal{V}
is a locally free module of rank 2 such that
\pi^{*}(\mathcal{V})=\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{1})
and
\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle with
\pi^{*}(\mathcal{L})=\mathcal{O}_{Y}(p_{1}\times E_{2})
. The first Azumaya algebra that we consider on
X
is the neutral endomorphism algebra
\mathcal{Q}:=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{V})
.

For later calculations, we want to describe
\mathcal{Q}
with a cocycle coming from
\mathbf{PGL}_{2}
and some cover of
X
. As in
Example

2.5
, set
E_{1}^{\prime}=E_{1}
and consider the fppf cover
m_{0}\colon E_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow E_{1}
which is multiplication by
2
. We have that
\mathcal{Q}_{1}=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}(\mathcal{V}_{1})
is split by this map, i.e.,
m_{0}^{*}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})\cong\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}^{\prime}})
. We consider two other maps related to this multiplication by
2
map on
E_{1}
.

First, let
Y^{\prime}=Y
and let
m\colon Y^{\prime}=E_{1}\times_{k}E_{2}\rightarrow E_{1}\times_{k}E_{2}=Y
be the map which is multiplication by
2
on the first factor. Second, there is a map
m_{X}\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
given by
m_{X}([a,b])=[2a,b]
, which is well defined since
m\circ i=i\circ m
on
Y
. Using the same techniques as in
[
GNR25
]
, one can see that
Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}\cong Y^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
and that under this isomorphism, the second projection
\textrm{pr}_{2}\colon Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
appears as

\displaystyle\beta\colon Y^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})

\displaystyle\rightarrow Y^{\prime}

\displaystyle((a,b),\gamma)

\displaystyle\mapsto(a+\gamma,b).

Likewise, we have that
X^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime}\cong X^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
with second projection

\displaystyle\beta_{X}\colon X^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})

\displaystyle\rightarrow X^{\prime}

\displaystyle([a,b],\gamma)

\displaystyle\mapsto[a+\gamma,b].

This is well-defined since
i(a+\gamma,b)=(-a+\gamma,b+t)
because
-\gamma=\gamma
for any
\gamma
of the
2
-torsion group scheme
\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
. Hence, these maps fit into a diagram

(31)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}\times\Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi\times\Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{X}}

which commutes when only the first projections are considered from the double arrows, as well as when the other maps are taken from each pair of double arrows.

Lemma 4.1
.

The quaternion algebra
\mathcal{Q}
constructed above on
X
is split by the étale cover
m_{X}\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
, i.e.,
m_{X}^{*}(\mathcal{Q})\cong\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}})
. In particular,
\mathcal{Q}
is defined by the
1
–cocycle

\displaystyle\phi=\Inn\left(\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
x&0\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&x\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\in\mathbf{PGL}_{2}\left((k[x]/(x^{2}-1))^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(X^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})).

Proof.

We begin by noting that all the schemes in the left column of (
31
) have the same global sections, namely
k[x]/(x^{2}-1)\otimes(k\times k)\cong\left(k[x]/(x^{2}-1)\right)^{2}
, and that the restriction maps along both
\pi_{1}\times\Id
and
\pi\times\Id
are the identity on those global sections. Likewise,
\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)=\mathcal{O}_{Y}(Y)=\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}(E_{1})=k
and the restrictions along
\pi_{1}
and
\pi
are the identity on
k
.

By definition,
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
is split over
E_{1}^{\prime}
and is defined by the cocycle

\phi=\Inn\left(\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
x&0\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&x\end{bmatrix}\right)\in\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(E_{1}^{\prime}\times_{E_{1}}E_{1}^{\prime}).

This means that
\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})
is defined by this cocycle pulled back along
\pi_{1}\times\Id
, which yields “the same” cocycle since the pullback along
\pi_{1}\times\Id
is the identity. We now consider the following diagram, the bottom two rows of which are from (
31
).

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}\times_{X^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times_{X}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m\times m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi\times\Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{X}}

First, we show that
\mathcal{Q}
is split over
X^{\prime}
. The algebra
\mathcal{Q}
is defined by gluing
\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})
on
Y
with the identity isomorphism over
Y\times_{X}Y
. Hence, the pullback
m_{X}^{*}(\mathcal{Q})
on
X^{\prime}
will be defined by the pullback of this gluing information. In particular, it will be defined by gluing
m^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}))
along the identity over
Y^{\prime}\times_{X^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}
. However, we know that

m^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}))=\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}}),

and so when we glue the matrix algebra to itself with the identity, we simply obtain the matrix algebra
\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}})
on
X^{\prime}
. Thus
m_{X}^{*}(\mathcal{Q})=\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}})
.

It is now almost immediate that
\mathcal{Q}
is defined by the given cocycle. Since
\mathcal{Q}
splits over
X^{\prime}
, it is defined by some cocycle
\psi\in\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(X^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}))
. Additionally, because
\pi^{*}(\mathcal{Q})=\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})
, the pullback of
\psi
along
\pi\times\Id
must be
\phi
. However, the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi\times\Id)^{*}\colon}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PGL}_{2}((k[X]/(x^{2}-1))^{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PGL}_{2}((k[X]/(x^{2}-1))^{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(X^{\prime}\times(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(Y^{\prime}\times(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}))}

is also simply the identity and so
\psi=\phi
. Hence,
\phi
defines
\mathcal{Q}
on
X
with respect to the cover
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
as claimed, and we are done.
∎

Corollary 4.2
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{V})
be the quaternion algebra as defined above on
X
. Then, the following hold:

(i)

\mathcal{Q}(X)=k
;

(ii)

there is a locally quadratic orthogonal involution
\sigma_{1}
on
\mathcal{Q}
;

(iii)

there exists a semi-trace
f_{1}
making
(\mathcal{Q},\sigma_{1},f_{1})
a quadratic triple.

Proof.

We know from
Lemma

4.1
that
\mathcal{Q}
is defined by the cocycle
\phi
over the cover
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
. Since we also have that
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})=k
and

\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}\times(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})}(X^{\prime}\times(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}))=(k[x]/(x^{2}-1))^{2},

we may repeat the calculations appearing in
[
GNR25
, 6.2]
.
∎

The second Azumaya algebra on
X
that we consider is constructed as follows. Let
p_{1}\neq s\in E_{1}[3](k)
be a nontrivial
3
-torsion point, so that there is a natural surjection
E_{1}\twoheadrightarrow E_{1}/\langle s\rangle
with kernel
\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}
. We consider the dual of these abelian varities and get another surjection
\phi:E=(E_{1}/\langle s\rangle)^{\vee}\rightarrow E_{1}^{\vee}\cong E_{1}
which has kernel
(\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z})^{\vee}=\mu_{3}
. Since we are in characteristic
2
, we have
\mu_{3}\cong\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}
and so
\phi
is a
\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}
-cover of
E_{1}
. Then, the cover

(32)

Z=E\times E_{2}\xrightarrow{\phi\times\mathrm{id}}E_{1}\times E_{2}\xrightarrow{\pi}X

is an
S_{3}=\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
-cover of
X
. Indeed, let
S_{3}
be generated by elements
\rho,\tau\in S_{3}
with relations
\rho^{3}=e
,
\tau^{2}=e
, and
\rho\tau\rho\tau=e
for the unit
e
of
S_{3}
. There is an action of
S_{3}
on
Z
over
X
given by

(33)

\displaystyle\mu:S_{3}\times_{k}Z\rightarrow Z,

\displaystyle\mu(\rho,(x,y))=(x+s,y)

\displaystyle\mu(\tau,(x,y))=(-x,y+t).

One can immediately check that the action (
33
) gives
Z
the structure of an
S_{3}
-torsor over
X
. Following
Remark

2.6
, we construct from this torsor a quaternion Azumaya algebra
\mathcal{P}
on
X
. An easy computation using the cocycle given in
Remark

2.6
and the facts that
\mathcal{O}_{Z}(Z)=k
and
\mathcal{O}_{Z\times_{X}Z}(Z\times_{X}Z)\cong k^{6}
shows that
\mathcal{P}(X)=k
. We also note that, since
\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{2})=\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
, this Azumaya algebra is neutral, i.e.,
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{W})
for a locally free sheaf
\mathcal{W}
of rank
2
on
X
.

Lemma 4.3
.

The algebra
\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}
has only constant global sections,
(\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P})(X)=k
. In particular, by
Remark

3.6
, the canonical quadratic pair
(\sigma_{0},f_{0})
on
\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}
satisfies the assumptions of
Theorem

3.5
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{Q}
is defined over the cover
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
and
\mathcal{P}
is defined over the cover
Z\rightarrow X
, we will be able to find a cocycle for the tensor product
\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}
on the fppf cover
X^{\prime}\times_{X}Z\rightarrow X
and use this to calculate the global sections. Therefore, we begin by identifying this fiber product. Just as
m\colon Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
and
m_{X}\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
are multiplication by
2
maps on the first factor, we can set
Z^{\prime}=Z
and define

\displaystyle m_{Z}\colon Z^{\prime}=E\times_{k}E_{2}

\displaystyle\rightarrow E\times_{k}E_{2}=Z

\displaystyle(e,e_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto(2e,e_{2}).

We then get a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{X}}

in which all square are cartesian, thus showing that
X^{\prime}\times_{X}Z\cong Z^{\prime}
. Since
Z\rightarrow X
is an
S_{3}
–torsor and
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
is a
(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
–torsor, the cover
Z^{\prime}\rightarrow X
is a
(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\times_{k}S_{3}
–torsor. Thus,
Z^{\prime}\times_{X}Z^{\prime}\cong Z^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\times_{k}S_{3}
and we obtain a large diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\times_{k}S_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\times_{k}S_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{X}}

where
f_{1}=(m_{Z}\times\Id)\circ\textrm{pr}_{13}
and
f_{2}=((\pi\circ\rho)\times\Id)\circ\textrm{pr}_{12}
. The map
\mu
was introduced above, the map
\beta_{X}
is the analogue of the map
\beta\colon Y^{\prime}\times(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
, and
\zeta
similarly applies the group action of
(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\times_{k}S_{3}
on
Z^{\prime}
. In particular, the maps
\beta_{X}
,
\zeta
, and
\mu
play the roles of the second projections. Therefore, the diagram above commutes if one considers only the first projections from each set of double arrows, and it also commutes if one only considers
\beta_{X}
,
\zeta
, and
\mu
instead.

Now, it is clear that
\mathcal{Q}
will be split over
Z^{\prime}
and therefore given by the pullback along
f_{2}
of its cocycle in
\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(X^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}))
identified in
Lemma

4.1
. Likewise,
\mathcal{P}
will be split over
Z^{\prime}
and be defined by the pullback of its cocycle in
\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(Z\times_{k}S_{3})
along
f_{1}
. We have that

\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\times_{k}S_{3}}(Z^{\prime}\times_{k}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\times_{k}S_{3})=\left(k[x]/(x^{2}-1)\right)^{2}\otimes k^{6}

and that

\displaystyle f_{1}^{*}=1\otimes\Id

\displaystyle\colon k^{6}\rightarrow\left(k[x]/(x^{2}-1)\right)^{2}\otimes k^{6},\text{ and}

\displaystyle f_{2}^{*}=\Id\otimes 1

\displaystyle\colon\left(k[x]/(x^{2}-1)\right)^{2}\rightarrow k^{6}.

This means that
\mathcal{Q}
is defined by the cocycle
\phi\otimes(1,1,1,1,1,1)
and
\mathcal{P}
is defined by the cocycle
(1,1)\otimes(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4},g_{5},g_{6})
where
g_{i}
are the elements of
\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
. Using the isomorphism

\displaystyle\left(k[x]/(x^{2}-1)\right)^{2}\otimes k^{6}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\left(k[x]/(x^{2}-1)\right)^{12}

\displaystyle(p_{1},p_{2})\otimes(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6})

\displaystyle\mapsto(p_{1}a_{1},p_{1}a_{2},\ldots,p_{1}a_{6},p_{2}a_{1},p_{2}a_{2},\ldots,p_{2}a_{6})

and setting

\phi_{1}=\Inn\left(\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
x&0\end{bmatrix}\right)\text{ and }\phi_{2}=\Inn\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&x\end{bmatrix}\right)

we obtain the cocycles

\displaystyle(\phi_{1},\phi_{1},\phi_{1},\phi_{1},\phi_{1},\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{2},\phi_{2},\phi_{2},\phi_{2},\phi_{2}),\text{ and}

\displaystyle(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4},g_{5},g_{6},g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4},g_{5},g_{6})

in
\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(k[x]/(x^{2}-1))^{12}
for
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{P}
, respectively. Hence, the tensor product algebra
\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}
is defined by the cocycle

(\phi_{1}\otimes g_{1},\ldots,\phi_{1}\otimes g_{6},\phi_{2}\otimes g_{1},\ldots,\phi_{2}\otimes g_{6})\in\mathbf{PGL}_{4}(k[x]/(x^{2}-1))^{12}.

Since
\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime})=k
, the restriction maps along both projections
Z^{\prime}\times_{X}Z^{\prime}\rightarrow Z^{\prime}
are simply the diagonal map
k\rightarrow(k[x]/(x^{2}-1))^{12}
. Thus, we have that

(\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P})(X)=\left\{B\in\Mat_{4}(k)\mid(\phi_{i}\otimes g_{j})(B)=B\text{ for }i\in\{1,2\},j\in\{1,\ldots,6\}\right\}.

Taking
B=[b_{ij}]_{1\leq i,j\leq 4}\in\Mat_{4}(k)
indexed as usual, we have that

(\phi_{1}\otimes\Id)(B)=\begin{bmatrix}b_{33}&b_{34}&b_{31}x&b_{32}x\\
b_{43}&b_{44}&b_{41}x&b_{42}x\\
b_{13}x&b_{14}x&b_{11}&b_{12}\\
b_{23}x&b_{24}x&b_{21}&b_{22}\end{bmatrix}

and so to be fixed,
B
must be of the form

B=\begin{bmatrix}a&b&0&0\\
c&d&0&0\\
0&0&a&b\\
0&0&c&d\end{bmatrix}=I_{2}\otimes\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}.

To then be fixed by all
\phi_{1}\otimes g_{j}
, the matrix
\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}
must be fixed by
\mathbf{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
, but only the scalar matrices are fixed this way. Thus
B=aI_{4}
for some
a\in k
and so we obtain that

(\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P})(X)=k,

as claimed.
∎

4.B.
Main theorem

We continue using the same notation as in the previous section, i.e.
k
is an algebraically closed characteristic 2 field,
X
is an Igusa surface realized as a quotient of a product of two ordinary elliptic curves over
k
, and
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{P}
are the two particular quaternion Azumaya algebras on
X
constructed above. We will need the following lemma:

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
k,X,\mathcal{Q}
, and
\mathcal{P}
be as above. Let
\tau_{3}=\mathrm{Spec}(k[x]/(x-1)^{3})
and
\mu_{2}=\mathrm{Spec}(k[x]/(x-1)^{2})
with canonical inclusion
\mu_{2}\subset\tau_{3}
. Then there exists a deformation
\mathcal{B}
of either
\mathcal{Q}
or
\mathcal{P}
to
X_{\mu_{2}}
for which
ob(\mathcal{B};X_{\tau_{3}}/X_{\mu_{2}})\neq 0
.

In other words, at least one of either
\mathcal{Q}
or
\mathcal{P}
has obstructed deformations.

Proof.

We write
(\mathcal{Q},\sigma_{1},f_{1})
for the quadratic triple associated to
\mathcal{Q}
by
Corollary

4.2
and
(\mathcal{P},\sigma_{2},f_{2})
for the quadratic triple associated to
\mathcal{P}
by
Remark

2.6
. Since both
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{P}
are neutral, we have
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{V})
and
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{W})
for two rank
2
vector bundles
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{W}
on
X
. Our proof works in two parts. First, we show that the obstructions to deforming either
\mathcal{Q}
or
\mathcal{P}
(or deformations of these) are equivalent with the obstructions to deforming
\mathcal{V}
or
\mathcal{W}
(or deformations of these) respectively. Second, we show that at least one of
\mathcal{V}
or
\mathcal{W}
has obstructed deformations by lifting the obstruction for the associated determinant bundles.

To see the first claim, note that by applying Serre duality, the exact sequence

\rH^{2}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{Q})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow\rH^{3}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})=0

is dual to the exact sequence

0\rightarrow\left(\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)^{\vee}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\vee}(X).

where the map
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\vee}(X)
sends a functional to its evaluation on scalars. Due to the isomorphism
\mathcal{Q}\cong\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
given by the reduced trace, any global section in
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(X)
is of the form
\Trd_{\mathcal{Q}}(x\cdot\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt})
for some
x\in\mathcal{Q}(X)
. Now, since
\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{Q})=2
,
\mathcal{Q}(X)=k
, and
\mathrm{char}(k)=2
, the evaluation of any such global section on a scalar will simply be

\Trd_{\mathcal{Q}}(x\left(c\cdot 1_{\mathcal{Q}}\right))=0

for
x,c\in k
. Thus, the map
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\vee}(X)
is identically zero, which going back through duality, means that
\rH^{2}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{Q})
is the zero morphism and we have that

\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{Q})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{O}_{X})

is an isomorphism. The same argument applies to show that the canonical map

\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{P})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{O}_{X})

is also an isomorphism. Since these maps can be considered as taking the obstructions to deforming the underlying vector bundle to the obstructions to deforming the endomorphism algebra of these bundles (see
Lemma

1.20
), we see that it suffices to show one of
\mathcal{V}
or
\mathcal{W}
has obstructed deformations. The remainder of the proof is devoted to showing this fact.

By construction, both
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{P}
have nonvanishing strong obstruction. By either direct analysis or the use of
Corollary

2.4
, we can therefore write
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{E}_{0}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{F}_{0}\oplus\mathcal{F}_{1}
for four rank
2
vector bundles
\mathcal{E}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{1}
on
X
. Further, we can assume that
\mathcal{E}_{0}
and
\mathcal{F}_{0}
are given as nonsplit extensions

0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow 0\quad\mbox{and}\quad 0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow 0.

We note that
\mathcal{E}_{0}\not\cong\mathcal{F}_{0}
since, if there was an isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cong\mathcal{F}_{0}
, then there would be an inclusion

\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\cong\mathcal{E}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{0}^{\vee}\subset\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\vee}\cong\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}.

However, the identity endomorphism of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
and the composition
\mathcal{E}_{0}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\subset\mathcal{E}_{0}
coming from the morphisms defining the extension of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
are two independent global sections of
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}(\mathcal{E}_{0})
and
(\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P})(X)\cong k
by
Lemma

4.3
.

Since we have
\mathcal{Q}(X)=k
and
\mathcal{P}(X)=k
, we find that
\mathcal{E}_{1}(X)=\mathcal{F}_{1}(X)=0
and
(\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{O}_{X})(X)=(\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{O}_{X})(X)=k
. There is then a commutative diagram with exact rows and vertical inclusions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

It follows that the first boundary square in cohomology

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}

sends
1\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{O}_{X})=k
to the class
\alpha(\mathcal{E}_{0})
in
\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{O}_{X})
of the given extension of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
. Similarly, the connecting map

k=\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})

sends
1
to the extension class
\alpha(\mathcal{F}_{0})
of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
. Note that since
\mathcal{E}_{0}\not\cong\mathcal{F}_{0}
, we must have that these classes are spanning, i.e.
\mathrm{Span}_{k}\{\alpha(\mathcal{E}_{0}),\alpha(\mathcal{F}_{0})\}=\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
.

Consider, now, the exact sequences

0\rightarrow(\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{trd}_{\mathcal{Q}}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow 0\quad\mbox{and}\quad 0\rightarrow(\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{trd}_{\mathcal{P}}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow 0.

These induce exact sequences in cohomology

\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{Q})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,(\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\vee})\rightarrow 0

and also

\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{P})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,(\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\vee})\rightarrow 0.

By Serre duality, the rightmost nonzero arrows in these sequences are dual to the inclusions of the
k
-span of
\alpha(\mathcal{E}_{0})
and
\alpha(\mathcal{F}_{0})
respectively.

By
Lemma

1.20
, the maps induced by the reduced traces

(34)

\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{Q})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{P})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})

may be interpreted as taking a deformation, of
\mathcal{V}
or
\mathcal{W}
to
X_{\mu_{2}}
, to their associated determinant line bundle. Since the image of either map in (
34
) is
1
-dimensional, and since these images lie in linearly independent directions, there is at least one deformation of either
\mathcal{V}
or
\mathcal{W}
that must have a determinant bundle with obstructed deformations to
X_{\tau_{3}}
, by
Remark

1.25
. Any such deformation of either
\mathcal{V}
or
\mathcal{W}
must then also have obstructed deformations to
X_{\tau_{3}}
. Setting
\mathcal{B}
to be the endomorphism algebra associated to such a deformation finishes the proof.
∎

Now, combining all of our work so far, we can prove:

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
(\sigma_{0},f_{0})
be the canonical quadratic triple on the tensor product Azumaya
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra
\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}
, cf.
Section

1.C
. Let
\mu_{2}=\mathrm{Spec}(k[x]/(x-1)^{2})
and
\tau_{3}=\mathrm{Spec}(k[x]/(x-1)^{3})
with inclusion
i:\mu_{2}\subset\tau_{3}
. Then there exists a deformation

(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
of
(\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P},\sigma_{0},f_{0})
to
X_{\mu_{2}}
such that

ob(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f;X_{\tau_{3}}/X_{\mu_{2}})\neq 0\quad\mbox{and}\quad ob(\mathcal{A};X_{\tau_{3}}/X_{\mu_{2}})=0.

In other words, the quadratic triple
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
on
X_{\mu_{2}}
does not deform to
X_{\tau_{3}}
, but
\mathcal{A}
does deform to
X_{\tau_{3}}
when considered as an Azumaya algebra alone.

Proof.

Let
\pi_{2}:X_{\mu_{2}}\rightarrow X
be the projection. Let
\mathcal{B}
be a quaternion algebra with obstructed deformations to
X_{\tau_{3}}
as shown to exist in
Lemma

4.4
. If
\mathcal{B}
is a deformation of
\mathcal{Q}
, set
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{B}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{P}
, and if
\mathcal{B}
is a deformation of
\mathcal{P}
, set
\mathcal{A}=\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{B}
. Equip
\mathcal{A}
with its canonical quadratic pair
(\sigma,f)
.
It is immediate that
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
is a deformation of
(\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P},\sigma_{0},f_{0})
.

To see that
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
does not deform to
X_{\tau_{3}}
, we use
Lemma

1.23
. Indeed, since
\mathcal{B}
does not deform, neither does
(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f)
. The obstruction
ob(\mathcal{A},\sigma,f;X_{\tau_{3}}/X_{\mu_{2}})
must then be nonzero. However, due to both
Corollary

1.19
and
Theorem

3.5
(or
Proposition

1.21
and
Corollary

1.22
noting
Remark

3.7
), all such obstructions vanish when one forgets the quadratic pair, i.e.
ob(\mathcal{A};X_{\tau_{3}}/X_{\mu_{2}})=0
.∎
